Boats arriving from Tangyang pay full inward dues if discharging at Shasi town, local trade dues if disg near the West station; but in the Shipmg table t all entered as local traders. In the samb way boats m Shasi town to Tangyang pay full outwar dues, but are classed among local elearances. Boats arnvmig from Tangyang for other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in tLe Shipping table first as local entries, then &quot; inteqport clearances. Boats arriving from other ports for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, as local elearances. 262 CTMSx GAZETTE. [JULY-SEPT., 1907&#39; NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 444. SiANGHAI DISTRICT. YANGTZE RIVER-IANGSHAN CROSSING. ALTEATIx IN POSITIoN OF Buoys. NOTICE is hereby given that, owing to changes in the Langshan Flats, the following alterations have been made in position of the buoys marking the Langshan Crossing:- North Bank Buoy is now moored in 49 feet at low water of spring tides. From the buoy, Fushan Pagoda now bears N. 78* 25&#39; W., distant 5.93 miles. Langshan Buoy is now moored in 58 feet at low water of spring tides. From it, Fusban Pagoda now bears N. 85&#39; o4&#39; W., distant 3.99 miles. Vine Point Buoy is now moored in 44 feet at low water of spring tides. From it, Fushan Pagoda now ars 8. 31&quot; W., distant 2.5 miles. Waterman Bank Upper Temtporary Spar Boy has been discontinued. All bearings given are magnetic. SHAxNA, i4th August 1907. No. 445. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. ENTRANCE TO THE WHANGPOO RIVER. ESTABLISHMENT OF WoosuNo SPIT GAS-LIGHTED BUOY. NOTICE is hereby given that the Woosung Spit unlighted buoy has been replaced by a gas-lighted buoy surmounted by a conical superstructure painted in red and black vertical stripes and showing an occulting red light every 8 seconds, thus:- Light . ........ . 4 seconds. Eclipse . . . . . . . . . 4 Y) The buoy is moored in io feet of water at low water of spring tides, and from it the WooSung Light bears S. 670 26&#39; W., distant I mile. SHANaHAI, 31st August 1907.
